In order to prove that these assignments are well defined, we use the fact that

iff

.

Claim 8
.

Let
p:\mathfun{L}A\to 2
be a complete homomorphism.
Then for all one-step axioms
\varphi
and assignments

\vartheta:V\to A
we have

p(\overline{\vartheta}(\varphi))=1\quad\text{iff}\quad W_{p}\in\vartheta^{t}(\varphi).

Proof 3.3

(Proof of claim)
.

We proceed by induction on the complexity of
\varphi
.
If

, where

is in one of the

, then we have

\displaystyle p(\overline{\vartheta}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad p(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\vartheta(v)\in W_{p}

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad W_{p}\in\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt(\vartheta(v))

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad W_{p}\in\vartheta^{t}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108ptv).

Let
\varphi=\top
. By definition,
p(\overline{\vartheta}(\varphi))=1
for all
complete homomorphisms
p
. Since
\vartheta^{t}(\varphi)=\mathfun{P}A
, the result
holds for
\varphi=\top
. For negation, we have

\displaystyle p(\overline{\vartheta}(\neg\varphi))=1

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad p(\neg\overline{\vartheta}(\varphi))=1

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad p(\overline{\vartheta}(\varphi))=0

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad W_{p}\notin\vartheta^{t}(\varphi)

(inductive hypothesis)

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad W_{p}\in\mathfun{P}A\setminus\vartheta^{t}(\varphi)=\vartheta^{t}(\neg\varphi).

Finally, if
\varphi=\bigwedge\varphi_{i}
then

\displaystyle\textstyle p(\overline{\vartheta}(\bigwedge\varphi_{i}))=1

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad p(\textstyle\bigwedge\overline{\vartheta}(\varphi_{i}))=1

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\textstyle\bigwedge p(\overline{\vartheta}(\varphi_{i}))=1

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad W_{p}\in\vartheta^{t}(\varphi_{i})\quad\text{ for all $i$}

(inductive hypothesis)

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad W_{p}\in\bigcap\vartheta^{t}(\varphi_{i})=\vartheta^{t}(\textstyle\bigwedge\varphi_{i}).

This completes the proof of the claim.

Now let
p:\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}A\to 2
be a complete homomorphism.
Then composing the quotient map
q:\mathfun{L}A\to\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}A
with
p

yields a complete homomorphism

p\circ q:\mathfun{L}A\to 2

(6)

whose kernel contains
\sim_{\Ax}
.
Moreover,
W_{p}=W_{p\circ q}
, where
W_{p\circ q}
is defined as in
(
5
) for the complete homomorphism from (
6
),
because
q
sends the generator
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pta
of
\mathfun{L}A
to the equivalence class
of
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pta
in
\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}A
.
Consequently,

W_{p}\in\vartheta^{t}(\varphi)\quad\text{iff}\quad W_{p\circ q}\in\vartheta^{t}(\varphi)\quad\text{iff}\quad(p\circ q)(\overline{\vartheta}(\varphi))=1\quad\text{iff}\quad p(\overline{\vartheta}(\varphi))=1.

Since
\overline{\vartheta}(\varphi)\sim_{\Ax}1

for all
\varphi\in\Ax
, we have
p(\overline{\vartheta}(\varphi))=1
for all
\varphi\in\Ax
.
Using the claim, this proves that
W_{p}
is an
\Ax
-subset.

Conversely, a similar computation shows that whenever
W
is
an
\Ax
-subset, then the kernel of
p_{W}^{\prime}:\mathfun{L}A\to 2

contains
\sim_{\Ax}
, and hence
p_{W}^{\prime}
defines a complete homomorphism

p_{W}:\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}A\to 2
.
In addition,
for each
b\in A
we have
p_{W_{p}}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108ptb)=1
iff
b\in W_{p}
iff
p(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108ptb)=1
,
so
p_{W_{p}}=p
. Similarly,
b\in W_{p_{W}}
iff
p_{W}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108ptb)=1
iff
b\in W
,
and hence
W_{p_{W}}=W
.
Thus, these assignments define a bijection.

Guided by Lemma
7
, we define an endofunctor on
\mathsf{Set}

which we then prove to be the Tarski-dual of
\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}
.

{defi}

Let
X
be a set and
\Ax
an increasing collection of one-step axioms.
Define
\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}X
to be the set of
\Ax
-subsets of

\mathfun{P}X
.
For a function
f:X\to X^{\prime}
in
\mathsf{Set}
, define
\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}f
by

\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}f:\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}X\to\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}X^{\prime}:W\mapsto\{a^{\prime}\in\mathfun{P}X^{\prime}\mid f^{-1}(a^{\prime})\in W\}.

If
\Ax
consists of a single axiom
\mathsf{ax}
, then we write

\mathfun{B}_{\mathsf{(ax)}}
instead of
\mathfun{B}_{\{\mathsf{(ax)}\}}
.

Proposition 9
.

The assignment
\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}
is a well-defined endofunctor on
\mathsf{Set}
.

Proof 3.4
.

Clearly
\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}X
is a set for every set
X
.
Let
f:X\to X^{\prime}
be a function and let
W\in\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}X
.
We need to show that
\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}f(W)
is in
\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}X^{\prime}
,
that is,
\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}f(W)
is an
\Ax
-subset of
\mathfun{P}X^{\prime}
.
But this follows from the fact that
\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}f(W)\in\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 0 L 3.57 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pta^{\prime}

iff
W\in\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 0 L 3.57 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108ptf^{-1}(a^{\prime})
.

Functoriality of
\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}
follows from the fact that
\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}
is a subfunctor
of
\mathfun{B}
.

We are ready to prove the main result of this section.

Theorem 10

(Tarski Functor Duality Theorem)
.

The functors
\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}
and
\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}
are Tarski-duals.

Proof 3.5
.

Define
\xi_{X}:\mathfun{at}(\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}(\wp X))\to\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}X

by sending an atom
a
corresponding to a complete homomorphism
p
to
W_{p}
.
This defines an isomorphism on objects by
Lemma
7
. We prove that the assignment

\xi=(\xi_{X})_{X\in\mathsf{Set}}:\mathfun{at}\circ\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}\circ\wp\to\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}

is natural by showing that
for every function
f:X\to X^{\prime}
the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfun{at}(\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}(\wp X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfun{at}(\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}(\wp X^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}X^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfun{at}(\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}(\wp f))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{X^{\prime}}}

commutes.
To this end, let
u\in\mathfun{at}(\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}(\wp X))
and let

p:\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}(\wp X)\to 2
be the corresponding complete homomorphism.
Furthermore, let
a^{\prime}\subseteq X^{\prime}
.
Then

\displaystyle a^{\prime}\in\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}f\circ\xi_{X}(p)

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\xi_{X}(p)(f^{-1}(a^{\prime}))=1

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad p(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108ptf^{-1}(a^{\prime}))=1

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad p(\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}(\wp f)(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pta^{\prime}))=1

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathfun{at}(\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}(\wp f))(p)(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pta^{\prime})=1

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad a^{\prime}\in\xi_{X^{\prime}}\circ\mathfun{at}(\mathfun{L}_{\Ax}(\wp f))(p).

This proves the theorem.

Corollary 11
.

For every increasing collection
\Ax
of one-step axioms, we have

\mathsf{Alg}(\mathfun{L}_{\Ax})\equiv^{\op}\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}).

{exa}

As the notation suggests, the functor
\mathfun{B}_{\varnothing}

defined as in Definition
3.3
using the empty set of axioms
is precisely the functor
\mathfun{B}
from Example
2.3
(
2
).
We have seen that its coalgebras are neighborhood frames.
Combining this with Example
3.2

and Theorem
10
yields that
\mathfun{L}
and

\mathfun{B}
are Tarski-duals.
As a consequence of Corollary
11
,
we derive Došen’s duality
[
Doš89
]
for neighborhood frames via

\mathsf{CANA}\cong\mathsf{Alg}(\mathfun{L})\equiv^{\op}\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{B})\cong\mathsf{NF}.

{exa}

Let
\Ax=\mathcal{C}_{\infty}
as in Example
3.2
.
For a set
X
, the set
\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}X
consists of all collections of
neighborhoods that are upward closed under inclusion and closed under
arbitrary intersections. Such a collection is uniquely determined by
its smallest neighborhood, and a straightforward verification shows that

\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}
is naturally isomorphic to the covariant powerset
functor
\mathfun{P}:\mathsf{Set}\to\mathsf{Set}
.

As a consequence of Theorem
10
, we obtain that
\mathfun{P}

is dual to
\mathfun{H}
, a result that was recently established
in
[
BCM22
, Theorem 4.3]
.
Using the well-known fact that
\mathfun{P}
-coalgebras are Kripke frames
and the observation that
\mathfun{H}
-algebras are CAMAs, we arrive at Thomason
duality:

\mathsf{CAMA}\cong\mathsf{Alg}(\mathfun{H})\equiv^{\op}\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{P})\cong\mathsf{KF}.

4.
Applications

In this section we first derive dualities for various types of neighborhood frames
using only one-step axioms and Corollary
11
.
This gives rise to Thomason type dualities for monotone neighborhood frames,
contingency neighborhood frames, and filter frames.
Each of these is an algebra/coalgebra duality.

Next we show how some of these restrict when we
invoke further axioms, which are not necessarily one-step axioms.
These results can be seen as correspondence
results. Most notably, they allow us to obtain McKinsey-Tarski duality for
topological spaces (with interior maps) as an easy restriction of the duality for
filter frames.
An overview of the dual equivalences discussed in this section
is given in Table
2
.

4.1.
One-step dualities

4.1.1.
Monotone neighborhood frames

Monotone modal logic is a well-studied branch of
modal logic (see, e.g.,
[
Che80
,
Han03
,
HK04
]
).
The standard semantics for monotone modal logic is given by

monotone neighborhood frames
.
Recall that these are neighborhood frames
(X,N)
such that
N(x)

is upward closed under inclusion as a subset of
\mathfun{P}X
.
We write
\mathsf{MF}
for the full subcategory of
\mathsf{NF}
whose objects are
monotone neighborhood frames.
It is well known that
\mathsf{MF}\cong\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{UpP})
, where

\mathfun{UpP}:\mathsf{Set}\to\mathsf{Set}
takes a set
X
to the collection of
subsets of
\mathfun{P}X
that are upward closed under inclusion
[
Han03
,
HK04
]
.

The algebraic semantics of monotone modal
logic is given by
monotone Boolean algebra expansions
(BAMs for short)

[
Han03
, Section 7]
.
A BAM is a neighborhood algebra
(A,\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0.71 0 C 0.71 0.39 0.39 0.71 0 0.71 C -0.39 0.71 -0.71 0.39 -0.71 0 C -0.71 -0.39 -0.39 -0.71 0 -0.71 C 0.39 -0.71 0.71 -0.39 0.71 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt)
such that

\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0.71 0 C 0.71 0.39 0.39 0.71 0 0.71 C -0.39 0.71 -0.71 0.39 -0.71 0 C -0.71 -0.39 -0.39 -0.71 0 -0.71 C 0.39 -0.71 0.71 -0.39 0.71 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt:A\to A
is a monotone function (that is,
a\leq b
implies

expressing monotonicity.
As a consequence of Theorem

, we have:

Corollary 12
.

\mathsf{CABAM}\cong\mathsf{Alg}(\mathfun{L}_{\text{\hyperlink{ax:M}{$\mathsf{(M)}$}}}).

For a set
X
, the
\mathsf{(M)}
-subsets of

\mathfun{P}X
are precisely the ones that are up-closed under inclusion.
It then follows immediately from the definitions that

\mathfun{B}_{\text{\hyperlink{ax:M}{$\mathsf{(M)}$}}}
defined as in Definition
3.3
coincides with

\mathfun{UpP}:\mathsf{Set}\to\mathsf{Set}
from
[
HK04
, Section 3.1]
.
As a consequence of Corollary
11
, we obtain:

Theorem 13
.

The category
\mathsf{MF}
of monotone neighborhood frames
is dually equivalent to
\mathsf{CABAM}
.

Proof 4.1
.

As a consequence of Theorem
10
, the functors

\mathfun{L}_{\text{\hyperlink{ax:M}{$\mathsf{(M)}$}}}
and
\mathfun{B}_{\text{\hyperlink{ax:M}{$\mathsf{(M)}$}}}=\mathfun{UpP}
are dual.
Moreover,
\mathsf{CABAM}\cong\mathsf{Alg}(\mathfun{L}_{\text{\hyperlink{ax:M}{$\mathsf{(M)}$}}})
by Corollary
12
,
and
\mathsf{MF}\cong\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{UpP})
by
[
HK04
, Lemma 3.4]
.
Therefore, Corollary
11
implies that

\mathsf{Alg}(\mathfun{L}_{\text{\hyperlink{ax:M}{$\mathsf{(M)}$}}})\equiv^{\op}\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{UpP})
,
which proves the theorem.

4.1.2.
Duality for neighborhood contingency logic

A formula is called
contingent
if it is possibly true and possibly
false. Otherwise it is non-contingent, i.e., it is necessarily true or
necessarily false. Neighborhood contingency logic was recently introduced in

[
FD15
]
to reason about contingent formulae, and is investigated
further in
[
BvDH17
,
Fan18
]
. The non-contingency modality
{\vartriangle}
is
interpreted in a neighborhood frame
(X,N)
by

x\Vdash{\vartriangle}\varphi\quad\text{iff}\quad\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket\in N(x)\text{ or }X\setminus\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket\in N(x).

As a consequence of this definition, the interpretation of formulae
does not distinguish whether
a\in N(x)
or
X\setminus a\in N(x)
or both.
Therefore, it suffices to only consider the neighborhood
frames
(X,N)
that satisfy: for all
x\in X
and
a\subseteq X
,

a\in N(x)\quad\text{iff}\quad X\setminus a\in N(x).

We call such frames
contingency frames
and write
\mathsf{ContF}

for the full subcategory of
\mathsf{NF}
consisting of contingency frames.
Then
\mathsf{ContF}
is isomorphic to the category of
\mathfun{B}_{\text{\hyperlink{ax:cont}{$\mathsf{(Cont)}$}}}
-coalgebras,
where
\mathsf{(Cont)}
is the axiom

\mathsf{(Cont)}

\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

.

Corollary

implies that

\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{B}_{\text{\hyperlink{ax:cont}{$\mathsf{(Cont)}$}}})\equiv^{\op}\mathsf{Alg}(\mathfun{L}_{\text{\hyperlink{ax:cont}{$\mathsf{(Cont)}$}}})

.

.

4.1.3.
Convex frames

Our next example concerns
convex neighborhood frames
. These are
neighborhood frames
(X,N)
such that
N(x)
is a convex subset of
\mathfun{P}X
,
meaning that for all
x\in X
, if
a,a^{\prime}\in N(x)
and

a\subseteq b\subseteq a^{\prime}
, then
b\in N(x)
. Write
\mathsf{CNF}
for the full
subcategory of
\mathsf{NF}
whose objects are convex neighborhood frames.

As we will see in Section
6
, convexity is closely related to
the question of functoriality of canonical extensions of neighborhood frames.
Convexity is captured by the following axiom:

\mathsf{(Conv)}

\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt(v\wedge v^{\prime})\wedge\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt(v\vee v^{\prime\prime})\to\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108ptv

Therefore,

\mathsf{CNF}\cong\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{B}_{\text{\hyperlink{ax:conv}{$\mathsf{(Conv)}$}}}).

We call the corresponding algebras

complete atomic convex algebras
,
and denote by
\mathsf{CACA}
the full subcategory of
\mathsf{CANA}
whose objects are
complete atomic convex algebras.
Then
\mathsf{CACA}\cong\mathsf{Alg}(\mathfun{L}_{\text{\hyperlink{ax:conv}{$\mathsf{(Conv)}$}}})
,
and as a consequence of Corollary
11
, we obtain:

Theorem 15
.

\mathsf{CNF}\equiv^{\op}\mathsf{CACA}
.

4.1.4.
Filter frames

Finally, we consider filter frames
[
Che80
, Section 7.2]
.
These are of interest because they
are as close as we can get to topological spaces using only one-step axioms (see below).
Recall that a
filter
on a set
X
is a subset
F\subseteq\mathfun{P}X
that is
closed under finite intersections and upward closed under inclusion.
In particular, this implies that
X\in F
as
X
is the empty intersection
of subsets of
X
.
A
filter frame
is a neighborhood frame
(X,N)
such that

N(x)
is a filter on
X
for each
x\in X
. Let
\mathsf{FF}
be
the full subcategory of
\mathsf{NF}
consisting of filter frames.

From the coalgebraic point of view, filter frames are

\mathfun{B}_{\Ax}
-coalgebras, where
\Ax
consists of the axioms

\mathsf{(N)}

\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\top

(\mathsf{C})

\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108ptu\wedge\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108ptv\leftrightarrow\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt(u\wedge v)

We write
\mathfun{B}_{\wedge}
for
\mathfun{B}_{\{\text{\hyperlink{ax:N}{$\mathsf{(N)}$}},\text{\hyperlink{ax:C}{$\mathsf{(C)}$}}\}}
.
We then have
\mathsf{FF}\cong\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{B}_{\wedge})
.
As a consequence of Corollary
11
, we obtain the dual equivalence

\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{B}_{\wedge})\equiv^{\op}\mathsf{Alg}(\mathfun{L}_{\wedge}),

(7)

where
\mathfun{L}_{\wedge}
abbreviates
\mathfun{L}_{\{\text{\hyperlink{ax:N}{$\mathsf{(N)}$}},\text{\hyperlink{ax:C}{$\mathsf{(C)}$}}\}}
.

Since
\mathsf{(N)}
and
\mathsf{(C)}
are the axioms that on the algebra side define modal algebras,

\mathfun{L}_{\wedge}
-algebras are simply modal algebras whose underlying
Boolean algebra is complete and atomic.
We write
\mathsf{caMA}
for the full subcategory of
\mathsf{CANA}
whose
objects are modal algebras based on complete atomic Boolean algebras.
By contrast, recall that
\mathsf{CAMA}
denotes the full subcategory of

\mathsf{CANA}
whose objects
(A,\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0.71 0 C 0.71 0.39 0.39 0.71 0 0.71 C -0.39 0.71 -0.71 0.39 -0.71 0 C -0.71 -0.39 -0.39 -0.71 0 -0.71 C 0.39 -0.71 0.71 -0.39 0.71 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt)
are such that

A
is complete atomic and
preserves arbitrary meets.
Therefore,
\mathsf{CAMA}
is a full subcategory of
\mathsf{caMA}
.
Rephrasing (
7
) yields the following generalization of Thomason duality:

Theorem 16
.

\mathsf{FF}\equiv^{\op}\mathsf{caMA}
.

Remark 17
.

If we require the collection of neighborhoods at each state to be closed
under intersections of size
<\kappa
, where
\kappa
is some fixed cardinal,
then we obtain the
\kappa
-complete neighborhood frames
from

[
Tan21
, Section 4]
. This corresponds to the axiom
\mathsf{(C_{\kappa})}
,
and yields a dual equivalence with
\kappa
-additive complete atomic modal
algebras
.
This category lies in between
\mathsf{CAMA}
and
\mathsf{caMA}
.

4.2.
Restrictions/correspondence results

If a collection of frames or algebras is not given by one-step axioms, we can
still derive dualities for them from correspondence
results for the axioms under consideration, but they are no longer
algebra/coalgebra dualities.
This allows us to derive other interesting dualities, such as
McKinsey-Tarski duality for topological spaces (and interior maps).

Topological spaces are obtained from filter frames by stipulating
the reflexivity and transitivity axioms
\mathsf{(T)}
and
\mathsf{(4)}
.
Adding only
\mathsf{(T)}
results in the more general notion of pre-topological spaces.
For a neighborhood frame
(X,N)
and
a\subseteq X
, let

\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0.71 0 C 0.71 0.39 0.39 0.71 0 0.71 C -0.39 0.71 -0.71 0.39 -0.71 0 C -0.71 -0.39 -0.39 -0.71 0 -0.71 C 0.39 -0.71 0.71 -0.39 0.71 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt_{N}(a)=\{y\in X\mid a\in N(y)\}.

{defi}

(1)

A
pre-topological space
is a filter frame that satisfies

\mathsf{(Cent)}

a\in N(x)
implies
x\in a
.

(2)

A
topological space
is a pre-topological space that satisfies

\mathsf{(iv)}

a\in N(x)
implies
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0.71 0 C 0.71 0.39 0.39 0.71 0 0.71 C -0.39 0.71 -0.71 0.39 -0.71 0 C -0.71 -0.39 -0.39 -0.71 0 -0.71 C 0.39 -0.71 0.71 -0.39 0.71 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt_{N}(a)\in N(x)
.

Remark 18
.

(1)

The above definition of topological spaces in the language of
neighborhood bases is well known (see, e.g.,
[
Wil04
, Theorem 4.5]
).

(2)

The above definition of pre-topological spaces can for example be
found in
[
SSSW02
, Appendix A]
.

(3)

Neighborhood frames satisfying
\mathsf{(Cent)}
are called centered

[
Lew73
, Section 1.3]
, hence the abbreviation.

In the language of topological spaces, neighborhood morphisms correspond to
maps that are both continuous and open. Such maps are often called

interior maps

[
RS63
, Section III.3]
. On the other hand,
continuous maps are the ones that satisfy only the left-to-right implication
of (
3
).

We write
\mathsf{PreTop_{int}}
for the category of pre-topological spaces and
interior maps, and
\mathsf{Top_{int}}
for its full subcategory consisting of
topological spaces. Clearly both
\mathsf{PreTop_{int}}
and
\mathsf{Top_{int}}

are full subcategories of
\mathsf{FF}
. Moreover, we have:

\mathsf{PreTop_{int}}\cong\mathsf{FF}\text{\hyperlink{ax:cent}{$\mathsf{(Cent)}$}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{Top_{int}}\cong\mathsf{FF}(\mathsf{Cent,iv}),

where
\mathsf{FF}\text{\hyperlink{ax:cent}{$\mathsf{(Cent)}$}}
and
\mathsf{FF}(\mathsf{Cent,iv})
are the full
subcategories of
\mathsf{FF}
whose objects satisfy
\mathsf{(Cent)}
and

\mathsf{(Cent,iv)}
, respectively.

The duals of topological spaces are given by complete atomic interior algebras,
and the duals of pre-topological spaces by complete atomic pre-interior algebras.

{defi}

(1)

A
pre-interior algebra
is a modal algebra

.

Remark 19
.

(1)

The dual concept of interior operator is that of closure operator.
Interior algebras were first introduced by McKinsey and Tarski

[
MT44
]
in the language of closure operators and under the
name of closure algebras. Rasiowa and Sikorski
[
RS63
]
called
these algebras topological Boolean algebras. The name interior algebra
is due to Blok
[
Blo76
]
.

(2)

Generalizing closure on a powerset to pre-closure yields the notion
of
Čech closure spaces

[
vFK66
, Definition 14.A.1]
.
This provides an alternate language to talk about complete atomic
pre-interior algebras.

Complete atomic pre-interior algebras are simply CANAs that satisfy

\mathsf{(N)}
,
\mathsf{(C)}
, and
\mathsf{(T)}
. We write
\mathsf{PreInt}
for the full subcategory of

\mathsf{CANA}
whose objects are pre-interior algebras. Then
\mathsf{PreInt}

is a full subcategory of
\mathsf{caMA}
. Let
\mathsf{Int}
be the full
subcategory of
\mathsf{PreInt}
consisting of interior algebras.

Given a neighborhood frame
(X,N)
a straightforward verification
(see, e.g.,
[
Che80
, Section 7.4]
) shows that:

(X,N)
validates
\mathsf{(Cent)}
iff its dual
(\wp X,\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0.71 0 C 0.71 0.39 0.39 0.71 0 0.71 C -0.39 0.71 -0.71 0.39 -0.71 0 C -0.71 -0.39 -0.39 -0.71 0 -0.71 C 0.39 -0.71 0.71 -0.39 0.71 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt_{N})
validates
\mathsf{(T)}
.

(X,N)
validates
\mathsf{(iv)}
iff its dual
(\wp X,\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0.71 0 C 0.71 0.39 0.39 0.71 0 0.71 C -0.39 0.71 -0.71 0.39 -0.71 0 C -0.71 -0.39 -0.39 -0.71 0 -0.71 C 0.39 -0.71 0.71 -0.39 0.71 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt_{N})
validates
\mathsf{(4)}
.

Thus, we arrive at the following duality theorems:

Theorem 20
.

The dual equivalence from Theorem
16
restricts to

\mathsf{PreInt}\equiv^{\op}\mathsf{PreTop_{int}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{Int}\equiv^{\op}\mathsf{Top_{int}}.

Remark 21
.

The object part of the dual equivalence
\mathsf{Int}\equiv^{\op}\mathsf{Top_{int}}

dates back to McKinsey and Tarski
[
MT44
]
, and the morphism part
to Rasiowa and Sikorski
[
RS63
, Section III.3]
. See
[
BH20
]

for more details.

Remark 22
.

Restricting Theorem
20
further gives rise to dualities for the categories
of
T_{0}
-spaces,
T_{1}
-spaces,
P
-spaces (that is, topological spaces
whose topology is closed under countable intersections),
and Alexandrov spaces (topological spaces whose topology is closed
under arbitrary intersections), with interior maps as morphisms.

Remark 23
.

Other topology-like spaces are the so-called
“generalized topological spaces” of Császár
[
Csá02
]
.
Proving correspondence results for the relevant axioms gives
rise to a duality for such spaces in a similar manner as for
pre-topological spaces.

The findings of this section can be summarized in the following diagram.
The horizontal arrows indicate full inclusions of categories.
The vertical arrows denote dual equivalences, and are labelled with the
relevant theorem or example.

5.
Jónsson-Tarski type dualties

In this section we derive categorical dualities for classes of neighborhood
algebras that are not necessarily complete and atomic. While this simplifies
the algebraic side of our story, it requires extra structure on the frame side
of the duality: we now have to work with
descriptive
neighborhood frames

[
Doš89
]
. For this we work with one-step axioms in the standard modal
language with finitary connectives. As corollaries we derive Jónsson-Tarski
duality for modal algebras and Došen duality for neighborhood algebras.

Our main contribution is to define an analogue of
\mathfun{B}
on Stone spaces by
modifying the celebrated Vietoris construction. As a result, we obtain a new
endofunctor on
\mathsf{Stone}
and show that the category of coalgebras for this
endofunctor is isomorphic to the category of descriptive neighborhood frames of

[
Doš89
, Section 2]
. The Vietoris space of a Stone space
\mathbb{X}
is
embeddable in this new hyperspace of
\mathbb{X}
as a closed subspace.

5.1.
Descriptive neighborhood frames

We start by recalling the definition of a descriptive
neighborhood frame, first introduced by Došen
[
Doš89
, Section 2]
.
However, to be in line with standard practice in modal logic, our definition of
general

frames deviates slightly from that of Došen in that
Došen’s definition requires that all neighborhoods
are admissible, while we view this as an additional
tightness
condition.
So our
tight general frames
correspond to Došen’s
general frames
.

Subsequently, we define a new endofunctor on
\mathsf{Stone}
generalizing the
Vietoris endofunctor, and show that the category of descriptive neighborhood
frames can be viewed as the category of coalgebras for this endofunctor.

{defi}

(1)

A
general
(
neighborhood
)
frame
is a tuple
(X,N,A)

consisting of a neighborhood frame
(X,N)
and a Boolean subalgebra

A
of
\mathfun{P}X
such that
A
is closed under

\lxSVG@closescope }

given by

\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0.71 0 C 0.71 0.39 0.39 0.71 0 0.71 C -0.39 0.71 -0.71 0.39 -0.71 0 C -0.71 -0.39 -0.39 -0.71 0 -0.71 C 0.39 -0.71 0.71 -0.39 0.71 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

(2)

A general frame
(X,N,A)
is
tight
if
N(x)\subseteq A

for all
x\in X
.

(3)

A general frame
(X,N,A)
is

differentiated
if for all distinct
x,y\in X
there is

a\in A
such that
x\in a
and
y\notin a
; and

compact
if whenever
A^{\prime}\subseteq A
has the finite intersection
property, then
\bigcap A^{\prime}\neq\varnothing
.

(4)

A
descriptive
(
neighborhood
)
frame
is a general frame that is
differentiated, compact, and tight.

(5)

A
general frame morphism
from
(X,N,A)
to
(X^{\prime},N^{\prime},A^{\prime})
is a neighborhood
frame morphism
f:(X,N)\to(X^{\prime},N^{\prime})
such that
f^{-1}(a^{\prime})\in A
for all

a^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}
. We denote the category of descriptive frames
and general frame morphisms by
\mathsf{DNF}
.

Let
(X,N,A)
be a descriptive frame. As usual, we can generate a topology

\tau_{A}
on
X
using
A
as a base. The space
(X,\tau_{A})
is compact
and Hausdorff because
(X,N,A)
is compact and differentiated.
Moreover, it is zero-dimensional because
A
is closed under complements,
and hence
(X,\tau_{A})
is a Stone space.
A subset
a\subseteq X
is in
A
iff it is
clopen in
(X,\tau_{A})
, so we can recover
A
from the Stone topology.

Since
A=\mathfun{clp}\mathbb{X}
and
N(x)\subseteq A
, it makes sense to
define a functor
\mathfun{D}
on
\mathsf{Stone}
that sends
\mathbb{X}\in\mathsf{Stone}

to
\mathfun{P}(\mathfun{clp}\mathbb{X})
. The choice of topology on

\mathfun{P}(\mathfun{clp}\mathbb{X})
is motivated by the desideratum to turn

\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}
into a Stone space, and is a generalization of the
Vietoris topology.

{defi}

The
\mathfun{D}
-hyperspace

\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}
of a Stone space
\mathbb{X}

is the space
\mathfun{P}(\mathfun{clp}\mathbb{X})
whose topology is generated by
the clopen subbase

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pta=\{W\in\mathfun{P}(\mathfun{clp}\mathbb{X})\mid a\in W\},\qquad\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

where
a
ranges over the clopen subsets of
\mathbb{X}
.
For a continuous function
f:\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
between Stone spaces,
define
\mathfun{D}f:\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}\to\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
by

\mathfun{D}f(W)=\{a^{\prime}\in\mathfun{clp}\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\mid f^{-1}(a^{\prime})\in W\}.

Lemma 24
.

The assignment
\mathfun{D}
defines an endofunctor on
\mathsf{Stone}
.

Proof 5.1
.

To see that
\mathfun{D}
is well defined, we first show that
\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}

is a Stone space.

.)
To see that
\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}
is Hausdorff, suppose that
W,W^{\prime}\in\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}
are
distinct. Then there must be an
a\in\mathfun{clp}\mathbb{X}
such
that either
a\in W
and
a\notin W^{\prime}
, or
a\notin W
and
a\in W^{\prime}
.

Finally, we show that
\mathfun{D}
is well defined on morphisms.
Let
f:\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
be a morphism in
\mathsf{Stone}
.
In order to prove that
\mathfun{D}f
is continuous, it suffices to show that

(\mathfun{D}f)^{-1}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 0 L 3.57 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pta^{\prime})
is clopen in
\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}
for all
a^{\prime}\in\mathfun{clp}\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
.
(The case for diamonds follows by working with complements.)
So let
a^{\prime}\in\mathfun{clp}\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
. Then

(\mathfun{D}f)^{-1}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 0 L 3.57 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pta^{\prime})=\{W\in\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}\mid a^{\prime}\in\mathfun{D}f(W)\}=\{W\in\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}\mid f^{-1}(a^{\prime})\in W\}=\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}

which is clopen in
\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}
. Consequently,
\mathfun{D}
is well defined.
Functoriality follows from the fact that
\mathfun{UD}
is a subfunctor of
\mathfun{BU}
,
where
\mathfun{B}=\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\mbox{\hskip 7.80904pt{1 0 -.55 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathfun{\mkern 3.5muP\mkern-3.5mu}$}}}}}{\reflectbox{\mbox{\hskip 7.80904pt{1 0 -.55 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\mathfun{\mkern 3.5muP\mkern-3.5mu}$}}}}}{\reflectbox{\mbox{\hskip 6.16771pt{1 0 -.55 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfun{\mkern 3.5muP\mkern-3.5mu}$}}}}}{\reflectbox{\mbox{\hskip 5.24484pt{1 0 -.55 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfun{\mkern 3.5muP\mkern-3.5mu}$}}}}}\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\mbox{\hskip 7.80904pt{1 0 -.55 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathfun{\mkern 3.5muP\mkern-3.5mu}$}}}}}{\reflectbox{\mbox{\hskip 7.80904pt{1 0 -.55 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\textstyle\mathfun{\mkern 3.5muP\mkern-3.5mu}$}}}}}{\reflectbox{\mbox{\hskip 6.16771pt{1 0 -.55 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfun{\mkern 3.5muP\mkern-3.5mu}$}}}}}{\reflectbox{\mbox{\hskip 5.24484pt{1 0 -.55 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfun{\mkern 3.5muP\mkern-3.5mu}$}}}}}
and
\mathfun{U}:\mathsf{Stone}\to\mathsf{Set}
is the forgetful functor.

Theorem 25
.

\mathsf{DNF}\cong\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{D}).

Proof 5.2
.

For isomorphism on objects,
if
(X,N,A)
is a descriptive neighborhood frame and
\tau_{A}
is the
topology on
X
generated by
A
, then
N
is a function from

\mathbb{X}=(X,\tau_{A})
to
\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}
which is continuous
because
A
is closed under
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0.71 0 C 0.71 0.39 0.39 0.71 0 0.71 C -0.39 0.71 -0.71 0.39 -0.71 0 C -0.71 -0.39 -0.39 -0.71 0 -0.71 C 0.39 -0.71 0.71 -0.39 0.71 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt_{N}
defined in Definition
5.1
(
1
).
Thus,
(\mathbb{X},N)
is a
\mathfun{D}
-coalgebra.

Conversely, a
\mathfun{D}
-coalgebra
(\mathbb{X},\gamma)
gives rise to the
descriptive neighborhood frame
(X,N,\mathfun{clp}\mathbb{X})
, where

X
is the set underlying
\mathbb{X}
and
N
is defined by

N(x)=\gamma(x)
.
Continuity of
\gamma
entails that
\mathfun{clp}\mathbb{X}
is closed under
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0.71 0 C 0.71 0.39 0.39 0.71 0 0.71 C -0.39 0.71 -0.71 0.39 -0.71 0 C -0.71 -0.39 -0.39 -0.71 0 -0.71 C 0.39 -0.71 0.71 -0.39 0.71 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt_{N}
.

The isomorphism on morphisms follows from a straightforward verification.

Remark 26
.

The Vietoris functor
\mathfun{V}
is a subfunctor of
\mathfun{D}
via the
natural transformation
\zeta:\mathfun{V}\to\mathfun{D}
defined on components by

\zeta_{\mathbb{X}}:\mathfun{V}\mathbb{X}\to\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}:c\mapsto\{a\in\mathfun{clp}\mathbb{X}\mid c\subseteq a\}.

This gives rise to a functor
\widehat{\zeta}:\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{V})\to\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{D})
,
defined on objects by sending
(\mathbb{X},\gamma)\in\mathsf{Coalg}(\mathfun{V})
to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfun{V}\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{\mathbb{X}}}

and on morphisms by
\widehat{\zeta}f=f
.
Specifically, if
(\mathbb{X},\gamma)
is a
\mathfun{V}
-coalgebra, then the
corresponding
\mathfun{D}
-coalgebra is given by
(\mathbb{X},N)
, where

N(x)=\{a\in\mathfun{clp}\mathbb{X}\mid\gamma(x)\subseteq a\}
.
The descriptive neighborhood frames lying in the image of
\widehat{\zeta}
are
precisely those descriptive frames validating
\mathsf{(N)}
and
\mathsf{(C)}
;
see also Example
5.2
.

5.2.
Functor dualities

Like we did in Section
3.3
, we can use one-step axioms to
prove that certain quotient functors of
\mathfun{N}
from
Proposition
4
(
1
) are Stone-dual to subfunctors
of
\mathfun{D}
.

Since
\mathfun{N}
is an endofunctor on
\mathsf{BA}
, we can only take quotients
with finitary axioms. These are axioms that have finite conjunctions
and disjunctions. Since this implies that axioms can only contain
a finite number of variables, it suffices to work with a countable set of
variables. Thus, we will work with the standard modal language, viewed
as a sublanguage of
\mathfrak{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
.

{defi}

A
finitary axiom
is a formula in the language
\mathfrak{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}^{\omega}

(defined as in Definition
3.1
).
A
finitary one-step axiom
is a formula in the language

(\mathfrak{L}^{\omega}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

.

We define assignments and satisfaction of these axioms in neighborhood algebras

. That is, an assignment for a neighborhood

is a function

and extends uniquely to

Like in Section
3
, for each
\mathfun{N}_{\Ax}
we can
define a dual functor
\mathfun{D}_{\Ax}
.
The functor
\mathfun{D}_{\Ax}
arises as a subfunctor of
\mathfun{D}
.
In particular, this means that for every Stone space
\mathbb{X}
,
the space
\mathfun{D}_{\Ax}\mathbb{X}
is a subspace of
\mathfun{D}\mathbb{X}
.

{defi}

Let
\mathbb{X}
be a Stone space and
B
its dual Boolean algebra of clopens.
For a finitary one-step axiom
\varphi
and an assignment
\vartheta:V_{\omega}\to B

of the variables we define the clopen neighborhood
\vartheta^{t}(\varphi)

recursively by

\displaystyle\vartheta^{t}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\displaystyle=\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
